Largan Precision (3008.TW)
08 October 2026 Citi Research
8
Largan Precision
Company description
Largan is a plastic/glass/hybrid lens supplier in Taiwan with end-applications
including phone cameras, DSCs, MFPs, and tablets. Its new development into
FAU business should server as a long term growth opportunity.
Investment strategy
We rate Largan a Buy as we expect its core iPhone business to remain resilient
and its co-packaged optics (CPO) development to provide strong upside

potential in the longer term. We believe Largan could be a long-term winner in
the lens market driven by: 1) its technology leadership; 2) intellectual property
(IP) protection; and 3) higher yield vs. competitors. In the longer term, we
believe spec migration could keep Largan ahead of competitors.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$8,500 for Largan shares is based on 33x 2027 /28E
EPS. We are using 2027 /28E EPS average as we see new Fiber Array + PMLA

revenue to kick in from next year. Our 33x PER is higher than its historical 5-
year PER range, which we think is justified by its better order visibility proven
by its recent strong capacity expansion plan, and we expect its CPO business
engagement should provide a strong long-term growth opportunity.
Risks
Key downside risks include: 1) weaker smartphone demand or replacement
demand, and in particular, demand headwinds for certain key clients; 2)

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-09

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: Given its longstanding position in the global foundry industry, 
ever challenging TSMC’s leadership during the late 1990s, we believe 
UMC stands well to benefit from a ripple effect in demand spurred by AI 
scaling, as detailed in the main section of this report. We expect UMC’s 
wafer loadings to reach 100% from 1Q27, with price hikes to go through 
(see Price hike to go through? Still likely, 31 July). That said, we believe the 
equity market has priced in this AI strength on extra volume and pricing 
power for UMC. Hence, we stick with our Hold (3) rating on the counter.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 preview and 4Q26 outlook. We expect UMC to 
report 3Q26 net profit of TWD16.4bn (+9% YoY), varying not much from the 
Bloomberg consensus. Its monthly revenue finished at c.TWD49bn QTD, 
reaching 65% of our/consensus 3Q26 estimates, which we consider 
tracking inline. For 4Q26, we expect UMC to earn net profit of TWD16bn, 
13% below consensus, as the street seems to be assuming higher non-
operating gains. UMC is scheduled to report results on 28 October; we will 
be offering updates in due course.

<=28nm ramp on AI scaling and fab optimisation. UMC’s 28/22nm-and-
below revenue contribution has hovered around the high-30% range over 
the past 2 years, after meaningful ramp during the COVID period on 
broadened demand applications from display drivers and TV SoCs to 
power management. With AI scaling creating a ripple effect in companion 
chip demand for connectivity (ie, silicon photonics) and power (ie, server 
PMICs), we believe UMC is well positioned to benefit from any spillover 
demand, with the strength augmented by TSMC’s fab optimisation, which 
could strengthen UMC’s pricing power. Given that UMC’s wafer loadings 
are likely to reach 100% from 1Q27, we expect its price hikes to go through 
and model increases of 2-5%, effective from 1Q27, across nodes related to 
AI applications. This should resume its <=28nm ramp to exceed a half of 
revenue in 2028. All in, we raise our 2027-28E EPS by 22%.

What we recommend: Despite our forecast revisions, we remain 
comfortable with our Hold (3) rating for UMC, as we believe its AI strength 
is already priced in. We raise our 12M TP to TWD145 (previous: TWD108), 
based on our higher ROE-adjusted PBR target of 4.2x (previous: 3.3x) 
applied to 1-year-forward BVPS on a higher ROE trajectory. Upside/ 
downside risks would be any price hikes above our expectations/ 
geopolitical overhangs (war, FX, interest rate) weighing on end-demand.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS do not vary much from consensus, 
indicating that UMC’s AI strength is already reflected in its valuation.

9 October 2026

United Microelectronics

A beneficiary of the AI ripple effect, but …

➢ UMC looks set to benefit from AI demand spillover 
➢ Yet we believe the AI ripple effect is being priced in 
➢ Maintain our Hold (3) call, with a higher 12M TP of TWD145

Company profile 
Incorporated in Taiwan, United Microelectronics Corp (UMC) is one of the leading semiconductor 
foundries in the world and second largest in Taiwan. UMC offers a series of foundry services from 
photo-mask, wafer fabrication to wafer probe & test. Its wafer fabs are spread over Taiwan, China and 
Singapore, with technology offerings ranging from 0.25u and above, 0.18/0.15u, 0.13u, 90nm, 65nm, 
45/40nm, 28/22nm to 14nm FF.

United Microelectronics (2303 TT): 9 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 8 Oct 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company 
ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
UMC’s Board of Directors consists of nine members whose expertise covers industry, academia,
finance, accounting, and management. Among these directors, six are independent directors,
representing 67% of total directors in 2024. The percentage of independent directors is high
relative to the regulatory requirement of at least 20% or at least 2 independent directors.
Although some of UMC's executive management are also directors, UMC's Chairman and
President are not the same person. Considering this and its relative-high percentage of
independent directors, we believe the board can perform its functionalities well
Capital management 2 It kept its dividend payout ratios above 50% in 2017-25, which is at a healthy, sustainable level.Overall, we see the balance between investment and dividend as appropriate.
Related party &
transaction 1 Sales to related parties were around 1-2% of its total revenue each year. The percentage isinsignificant and thus we see limited risk for UMC.
S Supply chainmanagement 2
UMC has constructed an evaluation framework for reviewing the sustainable development of its
supply chains based on UMC’s GG-026-004-C sustainable supply-chain management review
guidelines to ensure its suppliers fulfil environmental protection, social responsibility, labour and
human rights, safety & health and sustainable development guidelines. We believe these efforts
could reduce its supply-chain risk.
E Water & wastewatermanagement 2
UMC developed its “Water Management Policy and Commitment” in 2015 to promote more
effective utilisation of water resources. In line with this policy, UMC continues to enhance
recycling through increased use of reclaimed and desalinated water, ongoing wastewater
treatment R&D, and optimised discharge management. In 2024, the company achieved a 30.3%
reduction in water withdrawal per unit product, surpassing its 14% goal and meeting its 2030
target ahead of schedule, which demonstrates UMC’s strong commitment to water conservation.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
2
To achieve the goal of zero pollution, UMC has established a comprehensive new material
evaluation process to effectively screen and control new materials introduced into the
development of new processes. Through this effort, it can avoid or reduce the use of raw
materials and the associated environmental impact. UMC was the first semiconductor
manufacturer company in the world to obtain QC-080000 IECQ Hazardous Substance Process
Management (HSPM) certification for all its fabs by leading global semiconductor manufacturers
in completing the third-party verification and audit of its HSPM system in 2006.
E GHG emissions 2
UMC was the first semiconductor company in Taiwan to pass integrated environmental
protection, safety and health management system certifications in 2001. UMC has established
the UMC Low Carbon Commitment as a guide for future carbon reduction projects. Its GHG
emission reduction goals include: 1) to reduce fluorinated greenhouse gas emissions intensity by
65% over 2013-25 vs. 2010, and 2) to reduce electricity consumption intensity by 15% in 10
years over 2016-25 vs. 2015.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

during Aug, resulting in 3Q26 revenues up 74% YoY to NT$843m. With the gradual ramp 
up of clients’ new platform, we expect to see sequential MoM growth ahead, with Oct/ 
Nov revenues up 17%/ 19% MoM to NT$341m/ NT$406m, driven by a rising 
contribution from the LEO satellites.

Earnings revision: We factor in UMT 3Q26 revenues and revise down earnings by 1%/ 
1%/ 1% in 2026-28E mainly on lower revenues due to the momentum of product 
delivery, while we remain positive on the rising LEO satellite business exposure ahead.

Valuation: We continue to derive our target price based on a discounted P/E, and based 
on 2029E (methodology unchanged). We continue to derive the target P/E multiple from 
the average PEG&M ratio of peers in the satellite supply chain, and we remove outliers 
(telecom operators who face competition from LEO satellite operators). Our target 
2029E discounted P/E multiple is at 37.1x (vs. previously at 37.0x) which is based on 
peers’ avg. PEG&M, and UMT’s 2029-30E avg. NI YoY and OPM, due to higher avg. OPM 
in 2029-30E at 42% (vs. previously at 41%). We apply the 37.1x multiple to 2029E EPS 
and discount it back to 2027E via a COE of 7.8% (no change). Our 12m target price is at 
NT$2,549 (vs. previously at NT$2,557), implying 61x 2027E P/E, which is above the 
company’s average +1 stv. P/E of 40x, reflecting our positive view on UMT’s product mix 
upgrade toward LEO satellites. Maintain Buy.

### Page 4

Price target risks and methodology - UMT 
Valuation: We use a discounted P/E methodology and apply a 37.1x target P/E multiple 
to UMT’s 2029E EPS, discounting it back to 2027E at a COE of 7.8% (beta 1.2x, risk-free 
rate 1.6% and market risk premium at 5.1%), which leads to our 12-month target price 
of NT$2,549. The target P/E is based on the average PEG&M ratio of peers in the 
satellite supply chain. We are Buy-rated on UMT.  
Key Risks: Slower-than-expected LEO satellite deployment; Potential competition from 
new-entrant suppliers; LEO satellite operators manufacturing in-house components.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

Al infrastructure innovation is shifting toward rack-level co-
optimization, creating opportunities in optical interconnects,
advanced packaging, memory architectures, and power delivery
as agentic Al drives inference demand.

Greater China Technology Semiconductors
Asia Pacific
Industry View Attractive
CPO: Optical scale-up is getting closer to reality. A 64-XPU CPO-enabled
compute-rack demonstration highlighted the potential for direct optical links

between accelerators. As 200G/lane electrical channels approach physical
limitations, optical scale-up will become more important, particularly for customized
XPUs. However, pluggagle optics should retain scale-out volume leadership given
advantages in serviceability and cost. Packaging yield, fiber alignment, and reliability
remain key commercialization challenges.
Advanced packaging: Moving beyond 2.5D. MediaTek highlighted custom ASIC
designs approaching 10x reticle size, arguing that 3.5D integration could deliver

approximately 10x better die-to-die energy efficiency versus conventional 2.5D
interconnects. Management expects increasing adoption over the next 3-5 years.
MediaTek also emphasized custom HBM, proprietary die-to-die interconnects, and
DTCO optimization. We see structural opportunities for advanced packaging and
custom ASIC design services as system complexity increases (link).
Memory: Capacity matters alongside bandwidth. SK hynix introduced its MARS

disaggregated memory architecture, demonstrating a 3-5x improvement in time-to-
first-token through shared KV-cache access versus recomputation. Meanwhile, HBF
targets approximately half of HBM bandwidth but 14x higher capacity, with samples
expected in early 2028. Inference workloads will increasingly require heterogeneous
memory architectures encompassing HBM, DRAM, and NAND, potentially expanding
memory content beyond traditional GPU-attached HBM (link).

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
Optical interconnect: CPO is moving toward rack-scale scale-up, while pluggables
retain a scale-out advantage.
Custom ASICs and packaging: MediaTek sees 3.5D gaining traction as 2.5D
approaches physical limits
Memory architecture: SK hynix targets inference capacity and KV-cache
bottlenecks through MARS arid HBF.
Power and cooling: Racks above 200kW accelerate 800V DC, vertical power

delivery and liquid cooling.
October 13, 2026 04:27 PM GMT

Power and thermal: The next system-level constraints. Demonstrations of 6kw-
class double-sided cold plates vertical power delivery, and advanced microchannel
cooling highlighted intensifying thermal challenges. The transition toward 800V DC
should reduce conversion losses and improve power density. Meanwhile, AMD's
Helion agentic Al rack architecture reinforces incremental CPU demand alongside
GPU infrastructure. Power delivery, advanced cooling, and system integration

capabilities to become increasingly important differentiators.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O or Over) - The stock's total return is expected to exceed the total return of the relevant country MSCI Index or the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry
team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis over the next 12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E or Equal) - The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the total return of the relevant country MSCI Index or the average total return of the analyst's industry (or

industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis over the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR) - Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the relevant country MSCI Index or the average total return of the analyst's
industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U or Under) - The stock's total return is expected to be below the total return of the relevant country MSCI Index or the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry

team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Document 183: 9月外贸数据点评：出口超预期，结构改善加快

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月外贸数据点评：出口超预期，结构改善加快.pdf
Report source: 联储证券
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 1

9 月外贸数据点评：出口超预期，结构改善加快

[Table_Author] 
 魏争 分析师  沈夏宜 分析师

Email:weizheng@lczq.com Email:shenxiayi@lczq.com  
证书:S1320524100001 证书:S1320523020004

报告要点： 
出口明显超预期，增长的覆盖范围进一步扩大 。9 月，以美元计价，出口
同比增长 36.3%，较 8 月提高 11.3 个百分点，远高于万得一致预期
25.7%；环比增长 11.4%，也明显强于往年同期。出口超预期主要来自两
方面支撑，一方面，全球制造业景气继续改善，AI 产业链需求保持强劲。
9 月全球制造业 PMI 由 8 月的 52.3 升至 53.0，继续创近 4 年来新高，另
一方面，传统产业出口延续修复趋势，增长的覆盖范围进一步扩大，与 AI
产业链共同支撑出口增长。 
分产品看，机电产品仍是主要动力，传统商品修复明显加快 。一是，AI 产
业链维持高景气，优势制造业继续提供核心支撑。9 月，集成电路、自动
数据处理设备出口分别增长 136.0%和 92.9%，合计拉动整体出口增长
13.0 个百分点；汽车、家用电器出口分别增长 62.0%和 31.7%。机电产
品整体增长 43.7%，拉动出口 27.6 个百分点，贡献约四分之三的出口增
量。二是，传统商品普遍加快，出口修复的覆盖范围扩大。箱包、纺织
品、服装、鞋靴、家具和玩具出口分别增长 22.5%、19.0%、23.8%、
20.2%、22.0%和 40.8%，均高于 8 月。六类商品合计增长 23.0%，拉动
整体出口 2.6 个百分点，较 8 月增加约 1.4 个百分点。传统商品对出口的
支撑有所增强，但机电产品仍主导本轮增长。我们分析传统商品出口增速
改善有两方面原因，一方面，低基数放大了部分商品的同比修复。2025
年 9 月箱包、服装、鞋靴和玩具出口分别下降 12.3%、8.0%、13.3%和
28.0%。其中，本月玩具虽增长 40.8%，较 2024 年同期的两年复合增速
仅约 0.7%，或指向本月高增主要是对前期下滑的修复性回补。另一方
面，节日备货及交付前移可能影响月度出货。部分义乌圣诞用品商户今年
接单情况好于去年1；受生产、海运均需预留周期等因素影响，部分节日
商品提前安排交付，可能对近期玩具、箱包等消费品出口形成阶段性支
撑。 
分地区看，主要市场普遍改善，东盟和欧盟对出口加速的贡献尤为突出 。
一是，亚洲市场维持强劲增长，东盟贡献明显扩大。9 月，对东盟出口增
长 52.3%，较 8 月提高 22.1 个百分点，创 2022 年以来最高增速，其中，
对越南出口增长 53.3%；对中国香港、韩国分别增长 51.0%和 68.2%。东
盟、中国香港和韩国合计拉动整体出口 16.5 个百分点，较 8 月增加 3.9
个百分点，其中东盟拉动由 5.4 个百分点升至 8.5 个百分点。中国香港同
比增速虽回落，出口金额环比仍增长 18.1%。二是，对欧洲及其他主要市
相关报告  
9 月 FOMC：通胀回落仍不足，政策路径
整体偏鹰，AI 投资成为新的宏观变量 
2026.10.12 
美国 8 月 CPI：核心通胀超预期反弹，市
场重新定价利率路径 
2026.09.16 
8 月金融数据点评：财政与准财政加力有
望结束社融增速单边下行 
2026.09.16

### Page 2

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 2 
场出口明显改善。9 月，对欧盟出口增长 25.3%，较 8 月提高 18.6 个百
分点，拉动由 1.1 个百分点升至 3.7 个百分点；对德国、俄罗斯和加拿大
分别增长 31.4%、51.1%和 31.8%。三是，对美国出口继续修复。9 月，
对美出口增长 39.6%，较 8 月提高 5.2 个百分点，拉动整体出口 4.1 个百
分点；环比增长 12.7%，较上月明显加快。低基数仍是对美出口修复的重
要支撑。去年同期对美出口下降 27.0%，本月出口金额较 2024 年同期仅
高 1.8%，趋势性恢复仍需观察。 
从量价看，数量贡献增强、量价结构边际改善，整体增长仍保留较强的价
格驱动特征。一方面，传统消费品及部分装备产品的数量改善更为明显。
9 月，箱包和鞋靴出口数量分别增长约 11.5%和 10.1%，出口均价分别上
涨 9.9%和 9.2%，金额增长开始获得数量端支撑。家用电器、汽车和铝材
出口数量分别增长 25.4%、55.5%和 26.1%出口，均价分别上涨 5.0%、
4.2%和 16.3%，数量贡献较为突出。另一方面，电子产品仍具有较强的
均价驱动特征。集成电路出口数量仅增长约 1.1%，均价上涨 133.5%；手
机数量下降 12.6%，均价上涨 30.6%，价格仍是金额增长的主要支撑。在
跟踪的 11 类商品中，8 类呈现量价齐升，但船舶、液晶显示模组均价有
所下降，钢材数量基本持平。 
进口维持高增，机电产品仍是核心支撑，能源由拖累转为拉动。 9 月，进
口增长 31.0%，较 8 月提高 2.8 个百分点。结构上看，进口高增仍主要集
中于机电产业链，能源金额改善更多为体现价格效应。一是，机电产品拉
动继续增强，增量更多来自自动数据处理设备。9 月，机电产品进口增长
52.2%，拉动整体进口 21.6 个百分点。其中，自动数据处理设备增长
296.5%，拉动 8.9 个百分点，较 8 月增加约 2.8 个百分点；集成电路增长
74.7%，拉动 12.8 个百分点，其数量仅增长 1.7%，均价上涨 71.7%。二
是，能源价格支撑增强，带动金额表现改善。原油和天然气进口金额分别
增长 3.3%和 34.0%，均价分别上涨 31.5%和 36.3%，数量仍分别下降
21.5%和 1.7%。原油、成品油和天然气合计拉动进口约 0.8 个百分点，较
8 月拖累约 1.0 个百分点明显改善。三是，金属资源品表现分化，铜矿仍
主要由价格拉动。铜矿进口增长 27.1%，数量下降 4.2%，均价上涨
32.6%；铁矿石进口下降 7.4%，量价均有所回落。四是，农产品温和增
长，数量修复仍有限。农产品进口增长 5.5%，拉动整体进口约 0.5 个百
分点；大豆进口增长 2.8%，数量下降 7.7%，均价上涨 11.4%。 
往后看，出口仍有支撑，结构改善具备延续条件 。一方面，AI 投资及区域
制造业扩张继续支撑电子、设备和中间品需求；S&P Global 于 2026 年
10 月 9 日发布的全球贸易 PMI 分析指出，科技企业因担忧供给瓶颈和价
格上涨而增加缓冲库存，或可以为订单提供额外支撑2，但这部分需求的
持续性仍需观察。另一方面，部分传统商品及对美出口仍有低基数支撑，
贸易促进和摩擦管控有助于巩固订单。10 月 15 日开幕的广交会将为家
具、纺织、箱包、鞋靴和玩具等行业提供供采对接机会；10 月上旬中欧
磋商在汽车贸易等方面形成成果，有利于稳定相关市场预期 。往后看，一

### Page 3

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 3 
方面需关注 AI 产业链投资及相关出口需求的持续性；另一方面需关注传
统商品数量修复能否延续，以及提前交付对后续出货节奏的影响。

风险提示：全球制造业景气及外需超预期回落；全球 AI 投资需求不及预
期；贸易摩擦及出口限制超预期升级；地缘冲突超预期。

投资评级说明 
投资建议的评级标准  评级 说明 
评级标准为报告发布日后的 6 个月内公司股价 （或行业
指数） 相对同期基准指数的相对市场表现。 其中A 股市
场以沪深 300 指数为基准； 新三板市场以三板成指 （针
对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针对做市转让标的）
为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准。 
股票评级 
买入 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 10%以上 
增持 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 5%~10%之间 
中性 相对同期基准指数涨幅在-5%~+5%之间 
减持 相对同期基准指数跌幅在 5%以上 
行业评级 
看好 相对表现优于市场 
中性 相对表现与市场持平 
看淡 相对表现弱于市场

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
여전히 가격 상승 구간 
SK하이닉스의 26년 3분기 매출액은 95.8조원, 영업이익 75.1조원으로 예상한다. 
이전 전망 대비 감소한 수준이다. 원/달러 환율 영향이다. 영업이익률은 78.4%로 전 
분기 대비 2.1%p 상승할 전망이다. DRAM 매출액은 73.1조원, 영업이익 
59.2조원으로 추정한다. DRAM ASP는 이전 전망 대비 상향 조정했다. 일부 제품 
가격 인상을 반영한 영향이다. Bit Growth는 이전 전망 수준을 유지했다. ASP 
상향이 수익성 개선에 미친 영향은 크지 않을 전망이다. NAND 매출액은 22.4조원, 
영업이익은 15.9조원으로 예상한다. 
Global Top 수준 주주환원 규모/자사주 살 게 더 있다 
SK하이닉스는 2026년 8월 19일 주주환원에 대한 새로운 정책을 제시했다. FCF에서 
CAPEX를 제외한 금액의 50% 이상을 주주환원에 사용한다는게 핵심이다. 2026년 
기준으로 최소 90조원 수준일 것으로 추정한다. 자사주 중심으로 주주환원을 계획 
중이고 이미 집행 중인 40조원 이외에 4분기 중에 추가로 자사주 매입이 진행될 
것으로 예상한다. 배당도 일부 집행이 되겠지만 자사주가 중심이 될 전망이다. 
흔들리지 말아야 할 이유는 많다 
SK하이닉스에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. AI 모델의 성장으로 메모리에 
대한 수요가 지속적으로 높아지고 있고, 이에 비해 공급 증가는 제한적인 상황이 
상당기간 지속될 것으로 전망하기 때문이다. 이로 인해 현재 수준의 DRAM, NAND 
가격에 변동성은 낮고, 높은 수익성이 유지되면서 안정적 현금흐름 창출이 가능할 
것으로 전망한다. 현재 주가는 지나치게 저평가되어 있는 국면에 있다고 판단한다. 
목표주가는 12개월 예상 EPS 446,092원에 PER 9 배를 적용한 4,000,000원을 
유지한다.

Compliance Notice 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

종목명 담당자 담당자(배우자) 보유여부 1%이상 보유여부 유가증권 발행관련 계열사 관계여부 공개매수 사무취급 IPO 회사채 지급보증 중대한 이해관계 M&A 관련 수량 취득가 취득일 
해당 사항 없음

### Page 1

현대차 (005380)

EARNINGS PREVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.10.16  
4분기 반등 기대

3Q26 Preview 
현대차는 3Q26E 매출액 42.4조원(-9.4% YoY, -13.9% QoQ), 영업이익 2.5조원(-
2.8% YoY, -13.5% QoQ, OPM 5.8%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회할 전망이다. 각 부문
별 영업이익은 자동차 1.7조원(-2.0% YoY, -14.9% QoQ, OPM 4.8%), 금융 5,501억
원(-4.5% YoY, -27.5% QoQ, OPM 6.9%)으로 예상한다. 
생산 정상화 및 신차 확대를 통한 수익성 개선 기대 
현대차는 글로벌 도매 판매 91.8만대(-11.5% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 90.9
만대(-9.6%)를 기록했다. 7~8월에 발생한 파업 영향으로 약 5.5만대 규모의 생산 차
질이 발생했다. 파업 영향으로 HEV 판매 증가율이 1%에 그치면서, HEV 판매 확대에 
따른 믹스 개선 효과도 시장 기대에 미치지 못했다.  
4분기부터는 3분기 파업 영향으로 지연된 생산량 회복 및 신차 확대가 본격화되면서, 
전분기 대비 매출 및 수익성 개선이 기대된다. 동사는 보급형 전기차 아이오닉3의 튀
르키예 생산을 9월부터 시작했으며, 이를 통해 유럽 시장 판매량은 점차 회복될 전망
이다. 또한, 1) HMGMA를 통한 HEV 미국 현지 생산 확대, 2) 제네시스 HEV 모델 순
차적 출시를 통해 내년부터 북미를 중심으로 믹스 개선이 본격화될 전망이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 520,000원으로 하향 
2027년 영업이익은 11.2조원(+10.2%)를 기록할 것으로 예상된다. 원화 약세 지속을 
반영해 이익 추정치를 하향하며, 이에 따라 목표주가를 하향한다. 다만, 현재 주가는 
12M Fwd P/E 기준 6.1배로 Legacy OEM 평균(7.7배)보다 낮은 수준이다. 1) RMAC 
성과 구체화, 2) RA(로봇 생산법인) 착공 등 Physical AI 사업이 구체화될 경우 밸류에
이션 할증이 가능할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr 
Buy (유지)

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Stronger Growth, Costlier Money
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
The latest GDP report and annual revisions show a sturdier U.S. economy than previously
reported. According to the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA), real GDP grew at a 2.2%
annualized pace in Q2 compared to 1.5% previously estimated, and Q1 was revised
from 2.1% to 2.5%. Both 2024 and 2025 were also revised slightly higher. Most of the

first half of the year revision was in categories related to the Artificial Intelligence (AI)
buildout, but real consumer spending has also been revised slightly higher, accelerating
from 0.7% annualized growth in Q1 to 3.8% in Q2 despite surging energy prices and
slow hiring.
Spending in July and August remained much stronger than labor market conditions alone
would suggest, also averaging 3.7% annualized growth. Upside revisions to net interest

income and personal saving suggest households have benefited from more income than
was previously apparent, with personal saving revised higher back to 2023. The new data,
according to the BEA, show the January to July 2026 savings rate averaged 4.8% versus
3.3% prerevision, strengthening consumer spending underpinnings despite moderate
hiring, high energy prices and rising borrowing costs.
The BEA update also included inflation methodological changes related to categories

inflated by the AI boom—such as portfolio management fees and computer software and
accessories. These improved the inflation picture to some extent—August “core” PCE
inflation was reduced by 0.3 ppts to 3% YoY, bringing it closer to “core” consumer price
index inflation, which was 2.5% YoY and 2% on a three-month annualized basis. While still
above the 2% Federal Reserve (Fed) target, “core” PCE now appears stable at 3% year over
year since January rather than accelerating. These methodological changes are expected

to dampen pass-through of AI-related price pressures into “core” inflation and effects of
stock-market swings on inflation.
They also produced an encouraging improvement in recent PCE inflation momentum.
Three-month annualized “core” PCE inflation slowed from 4.3% at the peak of tariff
effects in February to 2.1% in August. For durable goods, this rate was down from 5.8%
in February to 1.5% in August, and, for services ex-energy and healthcare, it slowed from

4.2% to 2.2%, for example. Inflation is volatile on a three-month change basis, so it
remains to be seen whether this moderation continues without further Fed tightening.
For now, the trends in the San Francisco Fed’s cyclical versus acyclical inflation measures
diverged again in August. As expected, given softening wage growth, prices most sensitive
to the labor market have eased further. At 3% YoY, this inflation category is down from
8% in early 2023 and 4.6% two years ago is back to prepandemic levels and is likely

to ease more (Exhibit 1A). In contrast, supply bottlenecks, high energy prices and other
industry-specific factors have kept acyclical inflation on an uptrend. As shown in Exhibit
1B, the relentless increase in freight costs could prolong upside pressure on this category.
The diesel market has tightened in recent months, boosting its price by 70% YoY,
consistent with Brent oil at around $200 per barrel and large reverberations across
a wide swath of goods and services prices. A government-policy-related decline in

available truck drivers is also raising transportation costs more than expected. So are
tight ocean shipping capacity and longer/more expensive voyages due to the U.S./Iran
War. Supply-chain stress also increased as companies rushed to front-run potential new
tariff increases.
On the positive side for the inflation outlook, unit labor costs (ULC) are up modestly
as robust productivity growth has absorbed much of the wage growth, limiting a major
source of domestic inflation. Upside productivity growth revisions are likely to further

reduce ULC, which could help shift focus from energy and AI-related inflation to
eventual disinflationary effects once oil and refined fuel prices start to ease. Sustained
productivity gains would support growth and margins while limiting inflation and the
need for much higher interest rates.
Investment Implications
We favor selective Equity exposure
to companies with durable demand
and pricing power, while remaining
cautious about businesses most

exposed to fuel, freight, and
financing costs. We would also
gradually increase long-duration
exposure.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Surging nominal growth has boosted corporate revenues (up 13% YoY in Q2, according to the
BEA) while contained unit labor costs helped raise margins and profits more than expected.
With labor costs declining as a share of revenues, investment-related tax incentives and
rock-bottom net interest expense, pre-tax nonfinancial corporate profits as measured in GDP
accounts rose 21% YoY in Q2. Total corporate profits after-tax were up 18.2% YoY, according to

the BEA.
Surging AI-related imports and investment are keeping the business and profits cycle on
an uptrend both here and abroad. According to BofA Global Research, the global economic
cycle backdrop improved further in September, as reflected in a broad-based increase in its
Global Wave1 indicator. Broadening U.S. manufacturing strength, fueled by ongoing AI-driven
investment, a healthier orders backlog and low inventories suggest that the expansion has

room to run—a constructive environment for earnings. Typically, a Global Wave upswing is
consistent with rising earnings revisions ratios and equity price uptrends. According to the
Global Wave, global earnings growth expectations remain exceptionally strong.
Still, absent a resolution of war-related energy and transportation disruptions, the same
industrial strength intensifies inflation pressures. A prolonged supply shock raises the risk of
restrictive interest rates and weakening economic growth. In this context, the surge in the

10-year Treasury yield above 5% is starting to reflect more than optimism about U.S. growth
and strengthening productivity outlook. Oil’s tightest recent correlation with 10-year Treasury
yields in 35 years suggests a common energy-price thread behind rising yields in developed
countries.
Bond market spillovers from normalizing Japan yields and capital repatriation are another
common thread. Higher yields on foreign sovereign bonds such as in Japan—which has

escaped stagnation and deflation by intensely pursuing investment-led growth—weaken the
relative appeal of Treasurys. That distinction matters for portfolios. A yield increase driven
mainly by durable productivity gains would come with a stronger outlook for future income
and earnings. A yield increase driven by cost shocks and greater uncertainty raises financing
costs without the same benefit.
In sum, the economy remains more resilient than expected, but the bond market is also

charging more for an uncertain inflation outlook given untethered government debt and
cooling global demand for Treasurys spurred by geopolitical tensions and Japan capital
repatriation. The case for much further tightening becomes stronger if wage growth
strengthens, productivity lags, and unit labor costs reaccelerate, causing cyclical inflation to
also pick up steam. The case becomes weaker if that measure stays contained while fuel and
freight pressures recede or rising Treasury yields reduce asset prices and economic growth.

4 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Yields, Yen and Iran: What Haunts the “House”
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
We recently highlighted the three-front challenge facing Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent:
engineering a stronger yen, containing the 10-year Treasury yield and securing a diplomatic
resolution to the conflict with Iran.

Bessent recently has delivered one of the year’s more memorable lines while discussing
Treasury policy and the yen: “I have asymmetric information. I am the house now.” 2 Yet several
challenges remain: the 10-year Treasury yield is near levels last seen in the early 2000s, the
yen remains structurally weaker, and a phased agreement to reopen the Strait of Hormuz
appears remote. The resulting strain on energy markets keeps inflation risks elevated.

Put differently, oil prices, Treasury yields and the dollar-yen exchange rate are all trending
against Bessent’s objectives. Below, we examine how each variable is haunting his “house.”
Haunting the “House” is the Yen. Since the U.S. and Japan mounted a record $98.7B
coordinated intervention in late July after the yen weakened to a 40-year low near 164 per
dollar, the currency has appreciated roughly 4% against the dollar. The move was further
supported on September 18 when the BoJ raised rates by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%,

their highest level since the mid-1990s (Exhibit 2A). However, the split 7–2 vote and Governor
Ueda’s limited forward guidance leave the path of further tightening uncertain. The key
question is whether additional BoJ hikes can produce sustained appreciation in the yen, or
merely reinforce the temporary relief provided by the summer intervention.
Bessent, meanwhile, has repeatedly signaled his preference for a stronger yen. Partly because
yen stability is also a U.S. financing concern. Persistent currency weakness could prompt

Japan to support its currency by selling foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys—which would
be an unwelcome prospect as Washington partially relies on foreign capital flows to fund its
large fiscal deficits. That potential “haunts” the Treasury Secretary.  At precisely the moment
Washington needs foreign capital to finance its enormous fiscal deficits, it would be inopportune
for one of America’s largest creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities.

Exhibit 2: Charting the “Yentervention” and U.S. Treasury Yields Surge Across the Curve.

Haunting the “House” are Yields. The surge in yields runs counter to a White House intent
on lowering borrowing costs. The benchmark 10-year yield has broken above the 5% threshold
and climbed to levels not seen since 2007. At around 5.30%, it stands more than a full
percentage point above where it began the year (Exhibit 2B). The level is striking but so is the
speed of the move: Stronger-than-expected economic data, including a survey showing U.S.

2 September 8, 2026.
Investment Implications
While some key variables are
complicating the macro backdrop
(i.e., higher-for-longer yields/oil)
the combination of strong earnings
growth, robust consumer demand
(real final sales to private domestic
purchasers grew at 4.6% for Q2) and
ongoing capital investment suggests
that the fundamental backdrop for
U.S. Equities remains constructive.

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
business activity expanded at the fastest pace in more than five years, helped trigger an 18
bps surge in the 10-year yield—among its largest daily moves in decades. The 5-year yield also
topped 5% for the first time since 2007, while the 30-year yield reached 5.63%, its highest
level since 2002.
Compounding the move is inflation, running above the Fed’s 2% target for 67 months,

large fiscal deficits and the prospect of continued growth in Treasury issuance. Against this
backdrop, Treasury bought back $4.1 billion of long-dated debt, versus a maximum of $6
billion. That effort has done little to arrest the selloff. A subsequent auction priced nearly $4
billion of 7-year notes at 5.085%, the highest yield for that maturity in 33 years.
All in, the MOVE index, a key gauge of Treasury market volatility, jumped from 78 to 95 on

Wednesday September 23 and then to 104 the following day, its highest level since the early
days of the Iran war (Exhibit 3A).
Haunting the “House” is the Iran conflict. Early expectations that military pressure and
economic sanctions would quickly bring Iran to the negotiating table have yet to materialize.
Iranian officials have signaled that a full reopening of the Strait of Hormuz remains unlikely
while sanctions stay in place. Although talks between Washington and Tehran are continuing,

conflicting signals and lingering skepticism from both sides have kept tensions elevated.
As a result, oil prices have remained elevated in a range even as some Middle East supply
disruptions began to ease.
Startling is that Brent crude is up more than 60% this year, but refined product prices
have risen even faster. Ongoing disruptions to energy flows through the Strait of Hormuz,
combined with Ukrainian strikes on Russian refining infrastructure have tightened global

supplies of crude oil and refined fuels. Saudi Arabia has restored some drone-disrupted flows,
providing roughly 3.5 million barrels a day of relief (roughly 3% of global consumption).
The squeeze is mostly acute in diesel markets, where inventories remain constrained and
prices continue to climb (Exhibit 3B). U.S. diesel prices recently surpassed $6.50 per gallon,
the highest level on record, just as Russia has extended restrictions on diesel exports into

October. Against this backdrop, proposals to ban U.S. fuel exports risks worsening the
imbalance. According to S&P Global, an export ban could reduce refinery runs by nearly 2
million barrels per day and significantly cut gasoline production, potentially driving gasoline
and jet fuel prices even higher. As long as tensions persist, the oil impulse remains a key
impediment to structurally lower inflation.
Investment implications. Treasury Secretary Bessent increasingly sits at the intersection of

monetary policy, fiscal policy, foreign policy and national security. We remain constructive on
the U.S. economy and overweight U.S. Equities but are watching as these fronts challenge the
narrative. There is a great deal riding on the “house” versus the key macro variables of today.

6 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
When It Comes to Attracting Foreign Capital, the U.S. Remains Exceptional
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
There may be no better vote of confidence in a country than the willingness of
foreigners to put their money there. And by that measurement, the U.S. remains the
exception—both portfolio and FDI inflows are currently running at record highs based

on the latest data. This dynamic is one key reason why we remain constructive on U.S.
assets over the longer term.
Notwithstanding all the handwringing about America’s fiscal deficits, tariffs and rising
government debt, global investors sunk some $942B in net purchases of U.S. Equities in
the 12 months to July, the highest rolling 12-month total on record. In Q2, net purchase
of U.S. Equities soared to $426B, up 62% from the same period a year ago (Exhibit 4A).

By country, flows were down from China—not surprisingly—but strong from Taiwan and
South Korea, who have taken to recycling their massive AI-generated trade surplus back
into the U.S. In the end, strong portfolio inflows suggest that international investors
remain willing to increase their exposure to the world's largest capital market—that’s
bullish for U.S. assets.
Meanwhile, Exhibit 4B highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be

inside the U.S., with FDI inflows totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year.
That is a staggering 103% jump from a year ago and means FDI inflows are on track for
a record year, estimated at $480B for 2026.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Two, within the $32 trillion-plus economy with extraordinarily consumer

purchasing power, foreign firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment
than just trade (exports). Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers,
semiconductors, power generation, etc., has created numerous potential investment
opportunities for many foreign firms that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging
capex cycle. Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly
developed technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.

In the end, double-barreled capital inflows speak to the absolute and relative
attractiveness of the U.S. economy and capital markets. Stay invested in America—the
rest of the world is.

A) Net Foreign Purchases of US Equities and Investment Funds. B) FDI Inflows to the U.S.

is bullish for U.S. securities, both near-

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

ICE BofA MOVE Index is a market-implied measure of U.S. bond market volatility.
Consumer Price Index measures the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a market basket of consumer goods and services.
Baltic Dry Index is a daily shipping and trade index published by the Baltic Exchange in London that measures the cost of moving raw materials like iron ore, coal, and grain by sea.
Volatility Index (VIX) is a real-time market indicator that measures the stock market's expected volatility over the next 30 days.

Bank of America, Merrill, their affiliates and advisors do not provide legal, tax or accounting advice. Clients should consult their legal and/or tax advisors before making any financial decisions.
This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase
or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.
The Chief Investment Office (“CIO”) provides thought leadership on wealth management, investment strategy and global markets; portfolio management solutions; due diligence; and solutions
oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.
All recommendations must be considered in the context of an individual investor’s goals, time horizon, liquidity needs and risk tolerance. Not all recommendations will be in the best interest
of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.
Nonfinancial assets, such as closely held businesses, real estate, fine art, oil, gas and mineral properties, and timber, farm and ranch land, are complex in nature and involve risks including total

Document 392: Threats to European diesel imports_ risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased risk of price rises (2026-10-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Threats to European diesel imports_ risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased risk of price rises (2026-10-01).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 1st October 2026

EcoFlash

Threats to European diesel imports: risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased 
risk of price rises

In Europe, the possibility of a US ban on diesel exports has heightened concerns about price trends for this widely used 
fuel1, which has already risen significantly since the outbreak of the conflict in Iran. It has become a major political issue 
on both sides of the Atlantic in the run-up to decisive elections.

A particularly tight diesel market due to a shortage of buffer stocks 
Following the outbreak of the conflict, diesel prices did indeed see some of the sharpest rises on the oil market due to particularly 
tight market conditions. Since the end of February, whilst the price of a barrel of crude has risen by 46%, diesel prices have soared 
by 100% in Europe and by 76% in the United States.

The factors alleviating pressure on the refined products market, and more specifically the diesel market, were more limited than in 
the case of crude oil. Shipments passing through the Strait of Hormuz (around 17% of maritime diesel trade prior to the conf lict) 
have fallen by around 75%. Added to this is the halt in Russian exports due to war damage, which is depriving the diesel market of 
around 12% of its maritime exports. Furthermore, unlike with crude oil, China has not acted as a stabilising  force in the diesel 
market. On the contrary, the energy crisis has led to a halt in Chinese exports of refined products (including diesel) to the  rest of 
Asia from April onwards. The resumption of Chinese exports over the last two months remains fragile, given, in particular, that 
diesel stocks are at their lowest level in 15 months.

The United States has eased these tensions in the diesel market by increasing its exports of refined products (up 25% on average 
in the first half of 2026 compared with the average over the last five years). This rise in exports has required high refinery utilisation 
rate (close to maximum capacity) and, to a lesser extent, a reduction in stocks (-11% compared with the average over the last five 
years as at mid -September for middle distillates 2). All these factors have contributed to tightening the US domestic market and 
pushed retail diesel prices to record levels. Road diesel prices (US average) have risen by 68% since the end of February and by 
74% year-on-year.

A structurally high level of vulnerability in Europe… 
Against this market backdrop, characterised by mounting tensions and very limited room for manoeuvre , Europe would be 
particularly hard hit by a reduction in – or even a complete halt to – US exports. According to Eurostat, imports accounted for 46% 
of European demand for diesel in the first half of 2026, with 40% of these imports coming from the United States. Europe, which

1 Diesel accounts for 17% of the primary energy mix in the EU and 7.3% in the United States. 
2 A category of refined petroleum products comprising mainly diesel and aviation fuel.

### Page 2

has lost a great deal of production capacity in recent years, is operating its refineries at near -maximum capacity. Its room for 
manoeuvre is therefore limited.

… But the risk of a shortage has been ruled out in the short term 
The risk of a shortage, however, appears limited in the short term. The situation regarding European stocks is not comfortable, but 
it is not alarming. Commercial diesel stocks in the ARA region 3 are currently at historically low levels (around 12 million barrels), 
23% below their level at the end of September 2025. As for emergency stocks (EU Emergency Stocks), these are, according to the 
European Commission, at a sufficient level. Eurostat estim ates this figure at 270 million barrels in June 2026 (stable from a year 
earlier), representing approximately 60 days’ worth of EU consumption. Europe imports around 0.9 mb/d of diesel from the United 
States, and emergency stocks would therefore cover around 60 days’ consumption at 4.5 mb/d.

The rise in prices could be significant 
Whatever the outcome of the conflict, diesel prices are likely to continue to rise in the short term. According to our base -case 
scenario, crude oil prices are expected to remain very high in Q4 2026 due to ongoing tensions in the Persian Gulf, whilst a return 
to normal levels of diesel exports from Russia and the Middle East appears unlikely by that time.

Among the factors likely to cause future tensions, the start of the maintenance season in Europe could affect production leve ls, 
whilst demand for diesel in the United States is typically high in the autumn (agricultural harvests and pre -winter storage). I n a 
scenario where tensions remain low, the diesel refining margin (crack spread) could rise by 5% in Q4 compared with its level at the 
end of September 2026, before gradually falling back during 2027. An intermediate scenario (a 10% rise in the crack spread in Q4 
2026) would result in moderate constraints on US diesel exports and a drawdown of European stocks. Finally, an adverse scenario 
(a 35% rise in the crack spread in Q4 2026) would imply severe restrictions on US diesel exports and a delayed European response.

What impact will this have on inflation in the euro area? 
According to our estimates, the rise in diesel prices would exacerbate the expected peak in inflation in the euro area without altering 
its timing. In our baseline scenario for September 2026 (see our recently published EcoPerspective), headline inflation would peak 
at 4.1% y/y in January 2027 before gradually falling back towards 2% by the end of the year. The two least adverse scenarios 
would add only a limited amount to the peak: +0.1 pp in the first scenario (+5% for the crack spread in Q4 compared with the end 
of Q3) and +0.2 pp in the intermediate scenario (+10%), with inflation reaching 4.2% and 4.3% respectively in January 2027. 
However, there is an upside risk in the second scenario linked to the uncertain extent of the spillov er effects. In both cases, the 
main impact would be felt through fuel prices (which quickly reflect rising market prices) and would fade over the course of 2027. 
In the case of the adverse scenario (+35%), the additional inflation would be close to 0.6 pp by early 2027, pushing inflation above 
4.6%. A shock of this magnitude would, in fact, reduce businesses’ ability to absorb rising costs, which would lead to price increases 
across a broader range of goods (food, industrial goods). Subsequently, the effect would only fade gradually.

Pascal Devaux 
Economist, Middle East, Western Balkans – Energy 
pascal.devaux@bnpparibas.com

Guillaume Derrien 
Economist, Europe, Eurozone, International Trade 
guillaume.a.derrien@bnpparibas.com

3 Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp, Europe’s main hub for the storage of raw materials.

Document 470: こどもNISAから考える資産形成機会の差 ～政府はどこまで関与すべきか～

Source PDF: all report pdf/こどもNISAから考える資産形成機会の差 ～政府はどこまで関与すべきか～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

Research Report

2026 年10 月 13 日

1/7 
こども NISA から考える資産形成機会の差 
～政府はどこまで関与すべきか～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  奥田 宏二

（要旨） 
○ 2027 年に始まるこども NISA を題材に、子どもの資産形成を家庭の自助だけに委ねることの
限界と、政府が資産形成にどこまで関与しうるのかを論じる。ドイツを中心とする海外事例
も踏まえ、資産運用政策が「投資の促進」から「投資機会の提供」へ広がる可能性を検討す
る。  
○ こども NISA の資金を出すのは主に親だ。子どもを持つ割合が高い 30 歳代と 40 歳代の世帯
では、同じ年代でも金融資産の保有状況に大きな差がある。J-FLEC の 2025 年調査では、30
歳代の二人以上世帯で金融資産なしが 17.6％を占める一方、1,000 万円以上を保有する世帯
も 25.8％ある。40歳代でもそれぞれ18.8％、37.5％となる。住宅ローンなどの負担もあり、
こどもNISAに回せる資金は同じ年代でも各家庭で相当程度の差が生じると考えられる。 
○ ドイツは2027 年から、6歳から 18歳になるまでの子どもに政府が毎月10ユーロを拠出する
「早期開始年金」を始める予定だ。親が口座を開かなくても政府拠出分を運用する仕組みを
設け、家庭の資金力と親の行動に左右されにくい制度だ。米国、カナダ、英国でも政府によ
る資金拠出が行われてきた。ただ、その方法や成果は一様ではなく、英国のように政府拠出
を終了した例もある。  
○ NISAの普及によって投資しやすい環境は整った一方、実際に投資できる資金や機会には差が
存在する。この差にどう向き合うかが、資産運用政策の次のテーマの一つになるのではない
か。

1. はじめに 
2027 年1月から、少額投資非課税制度（NISA）の対象が子どもにも広がる。通称「こども
NISA」で、18 歳以上だったつみたて投資枠の年齢要件が撤廃され、0～17歳には年間60万円、非
課税保有限度額600万円の枠（注1）が設けられる。投資できるのは「長期・積立・分散」に適し
た投資信託だ。子どもの頃から資産形成をすれば、運用する期間を長く取ることができる。ま
た、運用による利益は非課税になるため、複利効果がより発揮されやすい。 
こどもNISAは、大学進学や成人後のライフイベントなどに伴う資金を準備できるようにするの

### Page 2

2/7 
が狙いだ。一方、実際に投資できるかは各家庭の資金力に左右される。自助を後押しするNISAを
巡り、「NISA貧乏」という言葉がSNSで広がった。NISA貧乏とは、投資に回す資金を捻出するため
に生活費を過度に切り詰め、日々の暮らしに余裕がなくなる状況を指す。この問題が子どもの資
産形成にも及べば、公平性の問題としてこれまで以上に意識されやすい。非課税投資枠を用意し
て投資しやすい環境を整えることと実際に投資できることには隔たりが存在する。この隔たりに
どう向き合うかが、資産運用政策の次のテーマの一つになると考えられる。 
子どもの資産形成支援は海外でも取り組みが進んでおり、ドイツでは 2027年に新しい制度の開
始が予定されている。新制度では6 歳から18 歳までの子どもに毎月10ユーロ（約1,800円）を
政府が拠出する。家庭の資金力にかかわらず、すべての対象者に資産形成の機会を提供する。本
稿では、こどもNISAを概観したうえで、ドイツを中心とする各国の事例から、子どもの資産形成
に政府がどこまで、どのように関与しうるのかを考えたい。

2. こどもNISA誕生の経緯 
こどもNISAは、新NISAのつみたて投資枠を未成年者にも広げる形だ。投資対象は長期・積立・
分散投資に適した投資信託に限る。 
NISAで未成年者を対象に制度を設けるのは、今回で 2度目になる。1回目はジュニアNISAとし
て、2016年に始まった。当時の政府には、NISAを若い世代にも広げるとともに、高齢世代に偏る
金融資産を子や孫の世代へ移したいという問題意識があった（注2）。 
ジュニアNISAは年間の非課税投資枠が80万円で上場株式や投資信託など幅広く投資できた。非
課税期間は当時の一般NISAと同じ最長5年間で、ロールオーバーなどで非課税のまま保有し続け
る仕組みだった。未成年者向けの制度として原則18 歳まで引き出し制限があった。 
ジュニアNISAは、利用実績が乏しいことなどを理由に延長されず、2023年末で新規投資を終了
した（注3） 。終了時点の口座数は約124万口座、累計買付額は1.26 兆円だった。その要因とし
て、制度が時限措置で投資できる期間が限定されていたことと、原則18歳まで資金を払い出せな
かったことがあげられる（注 4） 。子どもの年齢によっては十数年間にわたって資金が拘束される
一方、新規投資できる期間は限られ、非課税期間も原則 5年間とされていた。継続管理勘定など
を通じて18 歳まで非課税で保有できたものの、制度側と利用者側の時間軸がかみ合っていなかっ
たと考えられる。 
こうした課題は、解消・緩和されている。新NISAによって非課税保有期間が無期限となり、制
度も恒久化された。こどもNISAには引き出し制限がかかるものの、12 歳以降は資金を子どものた
めに使い、本人が同意することなどを条件に払い出せるようにする。例えば、進学に伴う入学金
や授業料に充てるといった使い方が想定される。 
使い勝手の改善とともに政府が配慮したのは「格差の固定化につながらないようにする」（注
5）という点だ。年間の非課税投資枠を60 万円、非課税保有限度額を600万円とし、成人向け
NISAより低く設定している。 
もっとも、非課税枠を抑えても、実際にどれだけ投資できるかは家庭が拠出できる資金に左右

### Page 3

3/7 
される。

3. 親世代の金融資産格差 
では、こどもNISAの資金を拠出するとみられる親世代は、どの程度の金融資産を持っているの
か。金融経済教育推進機構（J-FLEC）の2025 年調査で、未成年者の主な親世代と考えられる30
歳代と 40歳代の二人以上世帯をみると、金融資産保有額の中央値は30歳代で 311万円、40 歳代
で500 万円となる。さらに資料1で保有額の分布をみると、30 歳代では金融資産を保有していな
い世帯が17.6％ある一方、1,000万円以上を保有する世帯も25.8％を占める。40歳代でもそれぞ
れ18.8％、37.5％となっており、同じ年代でも金融資産の保有状況には大きな差がある。

資料 1 30・40 歳代の世帯金融資産保有額の分布 
30 歳代                                40 歳代

（注）金融資産には預貯金、株式、投資信託、保険商品などが含まれる。ただし、預貯金のうち日常的な出し入れ・引き落としに備
えている部分は含まない。無回答（30 歳代 2.6％、40 歳代 2.1％）は図示していないため、合計は 100％にならない。 
（出所）J-FLEC「家計の金融行動に関する世論調査 2025 年」より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

この世代は住宅ローンなどの負担も大きい。総務省の2025年「家計調査」で二人以上世帯をみ
ると、世帯主が30～39歳では貯蓄現在高1,073万円に対して負債現在高は1,940万円、40～49歳
でも貯蓄1,381万円に対して負債1,483万円だった。 
こどもNISAで年間60 万円という非課税枠の上限を月換算すると5 万円になる。親の収入から継
続的に拠出できる家庭、すでに保有する金融資産から資金を移せる家庭、少額の積み立てにとど
まる家庭などに分かれる。親に加え、祖父母から資金を出してもらえるかどうかによっても差が
生じる。こうした状況を考えると、こどもNISAを使うかどうかを含めて大きな差が生じそうだ。 
長期投資では、こうした投資資金の差がその後の資産形成にも影響する。同じ商品に投資し、

### Page 4

4/7 
同じ収益率を得ても、投資元本が大きければ得られる運用益も大きくなる。子どもの頃から長期
間運用すれば、その差が積み重なっていく。 
前述した通り政府もこの問題を意識し、未成年者の非課税枠を成人より低く設定した。ただ、
これによって抑えられるのは、資金力のある家庭が受ける税制優遇の大きさだ。投資するための
資金が少ない家庭では利用が進みにくく、子どもの資産形成にも差が生じるだろう。家庭の資金
力に左右されずに、子どもが資産形成を始められるよう政府が資金を拠出する制度を予定するの
がドイツだ。

4. ドイツはなぜ政府が初期資金を拠出するのか 
 ドイツ政府は2026年8 月、 「早期開始年金」の法案を閣議決定し、本稿執筆時点では審議中
だ。制度は2027年に開始する予定で、対象となる2020 年生まれの子どもには2026年分から遡っ
て拠出する。6歳から 18歳になるまでの子どもに対し、政府が毎月10ユーロを拠出し、資本市場
で長期運用する（注6） 。年金と名の付く通り、政府拠出分は原則65 歳まで引き出せない超長期の
資産形成支援制度だ。 
制度導入の背景には、子どもの資産形成が家庭環境に左右されているという問題意識がある。
ドイツ財務省は資産形成の機会が親の所得や家庭環境に左右され、その差が老後まで続く可能性
を指摘している（注7） 。家庭が自ら資金を出せるかどうかにかかわらず、政府の拠出によってす
べての対象者が資産形成を始められるようにする。 
ドイツ連邦銀行の調査によれば、投資信託や株式は資産の多い世帯で保有割合が高く、資産の
少ない世帯は預貯金など低リスクの資産が中心となる（注8） 。早期開始年金には、子どもの資産
形成を支えることに加え、長期的に資本市場への参加を広げる狙いもある。 
投資は、口座を開き、商品を選び、実際に資金を投じるという最初の一歩を踏み出すのが難し
い。早期開始年金では、親が投資に積極的かどうかにかかわらず、政府拠出によって子どもの資
産形成が始まる。すべての対象者が少額でも資本市場で運用される資産を持つことになり、最初
のハードルを政府が取り除く意味は大きい。 
さらに、親が口座を開かなかった場合でも、政府拠出分は連邦がまとめて資本市場で運用し、
ドイツ連邦銀行が管理する。政府拠出を受けるための申請も必要なく、親が手続きをしなくても
政府拠出が届く仕組みになっている。 
日本のこどもNISAとドイツの早期開始年金はいずれも、子どもの頃から長期投資を始めるとい
う点では共通する。一方、政府の関わり方には大きな差がある。日本は非課税で運用できる環境
を整えるが、資金の拠出は家庭に委ねている。ドイツは政府が少額とはいえ呼び水としての資金
を拠出する。子どもの資産形成をどこまで家庭に委ね、どこから政府が関与するのかという違い
が表れている。

5. 子どもの資産形成に政府が資金を拠出する世界の動き 
子どもの資産形成への政府による支援をめぐっては、各国が試行錯誤を続けている。米国では

### Page 5

5/7 
2026 年7月、18歳未満の子どもを対象にした「トランプ口座」が始まった。2025～2028年生まれ
の米国民には、連邦政府が1 人 1,000ドル（約16万円）を最初に拠出し、親や祖父母、親の勤務
先の企業などが追加で拠出できる（注9） 。政府が最初の資金を拠出することで、対象となる子ど
もは親が資金を用意できるかどうかにかかわらず、一定の金融資産を持つところから資産形成を
始められる。 
政府による資金拠出の方法は国によって異なる。カナダでは1998年から、教育資金を積み立て
る家庭に対して政府が原則20％を上乗せする仕組みが続いている。2005年には、低所得世帯の子
どもに対し、家庭が資金を拠出しなくても政府が最大2,000カナダドル（約22 万円）を拠出する
「カナダ学習債」も始まった。家庭の積み立てへの上乗せと、家庭からの拠出を必要としない支
援を組み合わせている（注10）。 
英国では2005 年に「チャイルド・トラスト・ファンド」が始まり、原則すべての子どもに政府
が初期資金を拠出し、低所得世帯には上乗せも行った。約630万口座が開設され、政府は累計で
約20億ポンドを拠出した。 
もっとも、政府が資金を拠出した後も家庭が追加できる資金には差が残る。チャイルド・トラ
スト・ファンドは低所得世帯への政府拠出を手厚くした一方、親族などによる追加拠出も認めて
いた。英国財政研究所は、所得の高い家庭ほど追加拠出額が大きくなるため、その子どもの口座
残高も大きくなる可能性を指摘していた。英国ではその後、財政再建の一環として政府拠出を縮
小・廃止した。2011年11月に始まった「ジュニアISA」は非課税で貯蓄・投資できる仕組みを引
き継いだが、資金は家庭が用意する形となった（注11）。

資料 2 子どもの資産形成を支える各国の制度

（出所）各国の公表資料より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

各国の経験からは、政府による資金拠出にも様々な方法があることが分かる。一律に資金を拠
出する方法、家庭の積み立てに上乗せする方法、低所得世帯への支援を手厚くする方法などだ。
一方、政府が資金を拠出した後も、家庭が追加できる資金には差が残る。英国のように財政負担
から政府拠出を終了した例もあり、政府拠出の効果や財政面での持続可能性も課題となる。

### Page 6

6/7

6. 「投資の促進」から「投資機会の提供」へ 
NISAの普及によって、日本でも投資は身近になった。それでも第3章でみた通り、30歳代と40
歳代でも2 割弱の世帯は金融資産を保有していない。 
この点で、ドイツの早期開始年金は、政府の資金拠出により、家庭の資金力にかかわらず、子
どもの頃から資産形成が始められる仕組みだ。すべての対象者が少額でも資本市場で運用される
資産を持つ意味は小さくない。 
こどもNISAが始まれば、親世代の資金力の差が子どもの資産形成の差としても表れ、資産形成
機会の差がより強く意識されるかもしれない。政府拠出には、財政負担をめぐる課題もある。し
かし、投資が身近になったからこそ、資産形成を始める機会を家庭の資金力や親の行動だけに委
ねない工夫が必要になってくる。資産運用政策の次のテーマとして、自助による「投資の促進」
に加え、資産形成の最初の一歩を支える「投資機会の提供」に向き合うべきだろう。

以 上

ロシアの7-9月期の長期金利は上昇した。中銀による金融緩和が進んだにもかかわらず、インフレ圧力の高まりや歳出
の膨張が嫌気されたものと考えられる。一方、同期のロシアの株価は下落後に横ばい。金融緩和が進んだものの、積極
的な買い材料に乏しいものと考えられる。

東京都港区虎ノ門5-11-2 オランダヒルズ森タワー

Document 503: 三季度4.5%的经济增速靠什么？

Source PDF: all report pdf/三季度4.5%的经济增速靠什么？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

国联⺠⽣宏观陶川团队认为四⼤拐点成关键⽀撑：⼯业⽣产提速、制造业投资⼤幅修复与
出⼝链条形成共振、基建实物⼯作量加速落地、消费受 " 以旧换新 " 与节假⽇错位共振反弹。
但内需基础仍不牢固 —— 居⺠收⼊增速落后于名义 GDP ，地产回暖尚需时⽇，政策能否
从 " 托底 " 转为 " 接⼒ " ，决定四季度⾛势。
摘要：
三季度 GDP 数据好于 IMF 预期，相当于阶段性证伪了其最新对中国经济全年下调的逻辑：经济并
没有沿着 “ 压⼒加⼤ — 增⻓失速 ” 的路径⾛，反⽽在政策发⼒后表现出缓冲。前三季度 4.6% 的经
济增速说明，下⾏压⼒存在，但政策托底正在产⽣效果，需求和⽣产的后劲仍有待挖掘。
当然 IMF 的预测证伪也不能当定论，四季度的经济企稳回升的不确定性并未完全消退。关键看政
策发⼒能不能从 “ 托底 ” 转成 “ 接⼒ ”—— 投资端要有实物⼯作量，财政和基建不能只看投放，还要
看项⽬落地；消费端也不能只靠⼀次性补贴，最终要看居⺠收⼊预期、就业和服务消费能不能
接续。
三季度 4.5% 的经济增速靠什么？我们可以结合 9 ⽉经济数据展现出的四⼤积极拐点加以理解：

⼀是极端天⽓扰动逐步消退、传统⽣产旺季带动，⼯业⽣产动能进⼀步增强。9 ⽉⼯业增加值环
⽐升⾄ 0.61% （前值 0.54% ），同⽐升⾄ 5.5% （前值 5.2% ），其中⾼技术制造业同⽐表现依然亮
眼，持续发挥绝对⽀撑作⽤。
⼆是制造业投资边际改善明显，中游装备制造与出⼝链条形成共振。9 ⽉制造业投资增速从 8 ⽉
的 -6.1% ⼤幅修复⾄ -0.9% ，结构上看，通⽤设备、电⽓机械、电⼦设备及交通运输设备等中游
板块成为主要拉动项。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

三是基建投资降幅收敛，重⼤项⽬实物⼯作量加速落地。9 ⽉基建投资降幅收窄⾄ -5.5% ，近期
⽔泥发运率、⽔泥产能利⽤率、沥⻘开⼯率等微观数据也都呈现明显修复， “ 六张⽹ ” 、 “ 两重 ” 等
重⼤项⽬资⾦正加速转化为实物⼯作量。
四是消费超预期回升，短期⽀撑有所增强。从结构驱动来看，中秋节假⽇的错位直接促成了节
庆服务消费的集中释放；前期 “ 以旧换新 ” 政策也在持续显效，为通讯器材等相关品类的⾼景⽓度
提供了坚实⽀撑。此外，受油价上涨的提振，⽯油及制品类零售额降幅明显收窄，多重因素共
振推动名义社零增速的边际改善。
当然，经济增⻓的可持续性不仅需要外需拉动，更取决于内需的实质性企稳。当前居⺠收⼊增
速仍阶段性落后于名义 GDP 增速，折射出内需修复的基础尚不牢固，客观上要求增量政策的进
⼀步加⼒提效。
着眼于此，我们预计后续政策博弈的重⼼将聚焦以下两⼤⽅向：从投资端来看，地产实质性改
善尚需时⽇。尽管 9 ⽉底已出台居⺠购房贷款贴息等政策，但受制于购房预期修复缓慢及城市、
项⽬间的分化，加之当前政策基调仍以 “ 去化存量 ” 为主，稳地产仍有待时⽇。后续住房公积⾦利

率等维度或存在进⼀步优化的空间。
从消费端来看，结构性冷热不均。当前的结构性短板在于居⺠⽀出更倾向于服务型消费，实物
消费尤其是⼤宗消费的动能依然偏弱；同时，消费端 “ 以价换量 ” 的特征依然明显，居⺠更追求

Document 533: 中选如何影响战争、财政和AI

Report source: 国金证券

中选如何影响战争、财政和
 AI 
基本内容 
今年 11 月3 日是中选日，Polymarket 显示民主党拿下众议院的概率超过90%， 拿下参议院的概率超过60%。 如果不出
意外，中选主要是民主党夺回众议院或者完成横扫两种结果，但同时在两院拿下超过2/3 席位数的概率很低。因此，
特朗普的总统否决权和军队指挥权依然强势， 中选结果在短期不太会对现有关键议题直接产生颠覆性影响。 中选的影
响在于，民主党可以驳回共和党的新立法提案和人事任命，并通过发起弹劾、调查、听证会等，给现有议题制造很多
麻烦，持续削弱特朗普和共和党的政治资源和外交影响力，从而影响战争、财政、AI 等关键议题的发展路径，以及
2028 年的大选选情。

风险提示 
1）近年选前民调的准确性有所下降，中选实际结果可能与当前各方评估存在较大差异；2）近年两党极化加剧，特朗
普行事风格难以预料，关键议题的实际演进路径可能受到预期之外的干扰。

### Page 2

宏观专题研究报告

敬请参阅最后一页特别声明 2

扫码获取更多服务 
今年 11 月 3 日是中选日，Polymarket 显示民主党拿下众议院的概率超过90%，拿下参议院的概率超过 60%。如果不
出意外，中选主要是民主党夺回众议院或者完成横扫两种结果，但同时在两院拿下超过2/3 席位数的概率很低。 
因此，特朗普的总统否决权和军队指挥权依然强势，中选结果在短期不太会对现有关键议题直接产生颠覆性影响。中
选的影响在于， 民主党可以驳回共和党的新立法提案， 驳回人事任命 （参议院） ， 并通过发起弹劾、 调查、 听证会等，
给现有议题制造很多麻烦， 持续削弱特朗普和共和党的政治资源和外交影响力， 从而影响战争、 财政、AI 等关键议题
的发展路径，以及2028 年的大选选情。 
一、“好戏”可能很快上演 
中选日之后，新一届国会履职要等到次年1 月 3 日，中间有 2 个月的过渡期，用于确认计票和解决争议。在此期间，
旧国会继续履职，两党将分别选举新一届领袖班子。 
过渡期并非垃圾时间， 反而是一段权力交接的敏感期。输掉控制权的政党寻求在权力交接前通过关键法律， 成功连任
的议员短期不再受制于选票，不再连任的议员可能会放手一搏，因此选举过渡期经常上演好戏。 
1998 年中选，众议院在过渡期通过了对克林顿的弹劾案；2018 年中选，特朗普要求在过渡期内拨付边境墙费用，导
致政府停摆 35 天；2020 年大选，临近过渡期尾声，特朗普拒绝承认拜登胜选，随后爆发国会山骚乱；2022 年中选，
民主党在圣诞节前夕压哨通过了2023 财年1.7 万亿美元的巨额拨款法案，包括对乌克兰的450 亿美元援助。 
由于今年中选大概率会发生权力变更，部分关键议题在过渡期就上演好戏的概率不低。 
图表1：民主党拿下众议院的概率超过90%，拿下参议院的概率超过60%

来源：Polymarket，国金证券研究所 
二、中选与战争：特朗普亲自决定命运 
回顾历史， 战争进程会直接影响中选结果， 中选结果又会间接影响战争走向乃至两年后的大选， 从而对美国政治和国
际局势产生重要的连锁式影响。 
1963 年肯尼迪遇刺后，民主党在 1964 年大选中大获全胜，因此虽然 1966 年越战升级令民主党在中选丢失了大量席
位，却保住了国会控制权。约翰逊在中选后继续升级战事，支持率持续下滑，直到1968 年 3 月宣布不再寻求连任。
共和党则重振旗鼓， 为1968 年尼克松赢得大选奠定了基础。 尼克松上任后， 为了在美越和平谈判中争取中国的支持，
促成了1971 年基辛格和1972 年尼克松访华。 
2006 年中选， 由于伊拉克战争， 小布什所在的共和党被横扫， 时任国防部长拉姆斯菲尔德辞职。 但是， 小布什仍然在
中选结束后不久宣布向伊拉克增兵，这进一步消耗了布什家族和共和党的政治资本，为2008 年民主党奥巴马胜选奠
定了基础。小布什的弟弟杰布·布什在十年后参加总统竞选时仍然深受两场中东战争的拖累，在党内初选被特朗普击
败。

### Page 3

宏观专题研究报告

敬请参阅最后一页特别声明 3

扫码获取更多服务 
今年的中选结果同样不会直接决定美伊战争走向， 但过渡期将是特朗普做出选择的时点。 局势可能会再动荡一段时间，
中选越不利于共和党，持续动荡的风险越低，反之亦然。 
由于民主党很难在两院赢下2/3 席位，只要特朗普愿意，他仍然可以强行维持甚至升级对伊朗的军事行动。但是，新
国会履职后，民主党可以通过限制国防拨款、引援《1973 年战争权力法》 、举行听证会等手段进行干扰，持续消耗特
朗普乃至整个共和党的政治资本，从而增加民主党在2028 年大选获胜的概率。 
因此，如果政治负担让特朗普难以在中选前退出，那么在过渡期内寻求体面退出，对特朗普家族和共和党都是更理性
的选择。 
具体路径可能更复杂， 例如特朗普可以选择在中选后同步推进军事冲突和外交谈判， 尝试通过冲突获得更好的退出条
件，这会让局势再动荡一段时间，也会进一步削弱共和党在 2028 年的选举前景。因此，中选结果对共和党越不利，
党内阻力和民主党的干扰力度就会越强，从而越快倒逼特朗普退出。 
要注意的是，如果特朗普在中选后选择直接军事介入也门冲突，局势将会更加复杂难解，短期退出的难度上升。 
三、中选与财政：参议院将产生有限影响 
历史上， 美国赤字率主要取决于经济形势和重大事件， 中选有影响但一般不是决定性因素， 而且需要长时间谈判和跨
党派合作。 
1994 年中选， 克林顿所在的民主党被横扫， 随后共和党通过预算案， 要求大幅削减支出， 克林顿则动用了总统否决权，
政府两度关门。 两党直到1996 年大选前夕才达成协议， 通过了 《个人责任与工作机会和解法案》 和 《平衡预算法案》 。
从 1993 年开始，美国赤字率逐年回落，甚至在1998-2001 年实现盈余，这固然得益于预算改革，更重要的是信息技
术革命让美国经济从80 年代的滞胀走向繁荣，冷战结束则让美国得以大幅削减国防开支。 
2010 年中选，奥巴马所在的民主党丢失众议院，共和党开始推动财政紧缩，导致2011 年债务上限危机，标普历史上
首次下调美国主权信用评级。 随后两党达成协议， 签署 《预算控制法案》 ， 严格约束了未来十年的可裁量支出上限。 从
2011 年到 2015 年，美国赤字率逐年回落，既与法案约束有关，也得益于经济危机后的复苏和超常规刺激政策退出。 
图表2：1970 年代以来美国赤字率走势和主要驱动因素（%）

来源：WIND，国金证券研究所 
近些年来，中选对赤字的影响更加不确定，例如 2018 年和 2022 年的中选均导致国会分裂，但次年赤字率都变得更
高，主要原因在于民粹盛行和两党极化。 
民粹压力使两党都不敢大幅削减福利支出，反而为了争取基本盘而轮番加码承诺，导致刚性财政支出压力持续上升。
回想马斯克 Doge 改革的黯然收场，就能理解削减财政支出的难度。实际上，这是民粹主义盛行的西方体系所面临的
共同问题。 
两党极化加大了跨党派合作难度，预算争议导致政府关门的时间越来越长，在民粹背景下，两党的妥协结果经常是双
方获得各自优先拨款， 导致预算进一步扩大。 此外， 税改等重要法案往往在中选前就已经通过， 即便中选后国会易手，
总统否决权也可以阻止大的改动。

### Page 4

宏观专题研究报告

敬请参阅最后一页特别声明 4

扫码获取更多服务 
今年中选，OBBBA 已经在 2025 年通过，即便民主党实现横扫，也难以大幅削减后续两年的财政支出；但夺回众议院
就能否定共和党的新增提案， 可能有助于避免赤字进一步扩大。根据国会预算办公室今年2 月的计算， 在基线情况下，
2025-2028 财年的赤字率为5.8%、5.8%、5.7%、6.0%，可见中选前后的赤字率差别不大，未来两年在基线基础上大幅
削减或者扩张的概率都不高，这对长端美债是一个中性信息。但根据10 月的估算，2026 财年实际赤字比年初预计多
出1000 多亿美元，再次说明如今财政赤字是易增难减。 
短期变数仍然在过渡期，参议院结果会有一定影响。2026 财年已经结束，维持联邦政府运营的临时延续决议将在12
月11 日到期，共和党需要力争在过渡期内通过对自己有利的2027 财年预算。之后还有“预算和解法案 3.0” ，目标
是在未来几年再增加950 亿美元支出，用于国防、情报、农业、选举安全等领域。 
目前预算案已经在众议院取得突破，过渡期通过的难度不大，丢失众议院不会造成明显影响。但参议院的进度缓慢，
而且需要 60 票，必须获得部分民主党人的配合。因此，如果共和党成功保住参议院，民主党的博弈空间不高，那么
法案在过渡期内通过的概率比较高， 明年的实际赤字会略高于基线； 如果共和党被横扫， 民主党将获得更多博弈筹码，
共和党需要做出更多有利于民主党的调整，否则可以僵持到新一届国会履职，届时政府将在年底停摆， “预算和解法
案 3.0”可能无法落地，明年的实际赤字将持平或略低于基线。 
四、中选与 AI：国会影响预期，地方影响现实 
笼统的说，AI 作为右翼科技路线，共和党人更倾向于宽松支持，民主党人更倾向于加强规范，而考虑到美国的政治
体制架构，实际情况会更复杂。美国作为United States，国家是由各州联合起来的，各州在让渡部分权力给联邦政
府的同时， 也有相当大的自主权。 因此， 对于AI 产业， 全国性的监管框架、 综合立法、 对科技公司的监督调查等， 权
力在联邦政府，即国会层面；基础设施建设，包括土地分区、环境与水资源许可、电网审批等，权力在地方政府，即
州长、州议会层面。中选既有国会选举，也有地方选举，对AI 的影响更复杂。 
看未来两年， 国会选举对基础模型开发商和云厂商的现实影响有限， 主要是在预期层面， 地方选举则可能对基础设施
建设造成一定现实影响。 
国会选举影响 AI 监管预期。特朗普是AI 的大力支持者，掌握总统否决权，所以即便民主党实现横扫，短期也很难在
联邦层面通过大幅限制AI 发展的立法。但是从预期的角度，民主党在中选的优势越明显，赢得2028 年大选的可能性
就越值得关注，反之亦然。要说明的是，历史上中选结果与两年后的大选结果相关性较低，民主党的计划可能是通过
发起对特朗普的弹劾和调查，揭露对共和党不利的信息，虽然不太可能弹劾成功，但目标是增加2028 年的胜算。 
如果预期民主党赢得 2028 年大选，可能会加强对模型开发商的审计和测试，提高合规成本、延长研发周期；在联邦
层面调整对基础设施建设相关的物理条件审批、 电价分担规则、 税收规则等， 导致建设节奏放缓、 成本上升； 在医疗、
金融等应用端制定行业规则，加强数据治理、隐私保护等。 
地方选举影响 AIDC 建设现实。今年中选共有 36 个州选举州长，共和党和民主党各持有18 个。由于两党民众都普遍
对数据中心建设持有负面意见，无论是哪个党派的候选人，无条件支持数据中心建设的立场已经较为少见，而且两党
都有不少立场激进的候选人。相对激进的主张是支持全州暂停新的数据中心建设，直到制定更好的监管框架，相对温
和的主张则是支持加强监管和取消优惠。切入点包括要求开发商承担更多公用事业成本、降低居民电费、提高空气和
水质等环境标准、暂停或结束对数据中心的税收优惠等。 
因此， 加强对数据中心建设的约束在地方层面已经是大势所趋， 本质上会提高数据中心的综合建设成本、 降低建设效
率，主要变量是候选人立场差异较大的州的竞选情况。根据Distilled 的统计，计划建设数据中心规模较大、且选举
有一定竞争性的有10 个州，共涉及67.4GW 的计划建设数据中心，与美国当前已在运营的数据中心容量相当。

### Page 5

宏观专题研究报告

敬请参阅最后一页特别声明 5

扫码获取更多服务 
图表3：竞争激烈的 10 个州，涉及的计划建设数据中心容量达到67.4GW

全球⼗⼤財經事件｜ 2026/10/14 07:50 （台灣時間）
涵蓋區間：台灣時間  10/11 07:50 ⾄  10/14 07:50 （近 72 ⼩時）。本期⼊選 10 件：近 12 ⼩時 5 件、 12 ⾄
48 ⼩時 5 件、 48 ⾄ 72 ⼩時 0 件。
事件  1
10/13 紐約聯儲總裁威廉斯：年內再升息⼀次合適， 2027 年預期無須再升（台
灣時間  10/14 02:07 ）
五⼤重點
1  FOMC （聯邦公開市場委員會）副主席、紐約聯儲總裁威廉斯 10/13 受訪表⽰，今年再升息⼀
次是合適的，但⽬前預期 2027 年沒有升息必要。
2   他說 9 ⽉升息的理由「始於⾵險管理」並堅定⽀持；通膨明年將⼤幅放緩、接近 2% ⽬標，除 AI

與能源外未⾒普遍的價格壓⼒，勞動市場也未推升通膨。
3   他點名 AI 是⽬前最密切關注的通膨驅動因素；同⽇波⼠頓聯儲總裁柯林斯也表⽰年底前可能需
再升息，並指資料中⼼建設正推⾼通膨數據。
4   談話後 10 年期美債殖利率下跌 2.51 個基點⾄ 5.219% ，美元指數收跌 0.4% ⾄ 102.18 ；標普 500
指數收跌 0.02% ，幾乎收平。
5  CME FedWatch （ 10/13 早盤）顯⽰ 10 ⽉按兵不動機率 79.5% 、 12 ⽉前累計升息 1 碼機率
70.3% ；美國 9 ⽉ CPI （消費者物價指數）預定台灣時間 10/14 20:30 公布。
三⼤結論
1   威廉斯是常任票委，「再升⼀次後停⽌」勾勒出終點利率約 4.00% ⾄ 4.25% ，與 9 ⽉點陣圖中位
數⼀致；暫停不等於轉向寬鬆，短天期國庫券仍是承接政策不確定性的⾸選。
2   他把通膨壓⼒限縮在 AI 與能源，代表聯準會在意的是集中型⽽⾮全⾯型通膨；當 9 ⽉ CPI 核⼼
服務與能源同步⾛⾼時，應預期 12 ⽉升息定價升⾼，降低⾼估值成⻑股與⻑天期公債。

### Page 2

3  10 年期殖利率仍在 5.2% 上⽅，⻑端反映財政供給與期限溢價⽽⾮僅是政策路徑；當核⼼ PCE
三個⽉年化維持約 2% 且 10 年期殖利率停⽌創⾼時，才逐步把到期短券轉向⾼品質股票。
事件  2
10/13 美國⼤型銀⾏第三季財報：摩根⼤通營收低於預期，花旗收跌 5.6% （台
灣時間  10/13 20:30 ）
五⼤重點
1   摩根⼤通第三季淨利 153 億美元、每股盈餘 5.57 美元（去年同期 5.07 美元），營收約 502 億美
元；固定收益交易年減 4% ，股票交易年增 49% 、為史上第⼆⾼。
2   摩根⼤通將 2026 年淨利息收⼊指引上調⾄ 1,062 億美元、費⽤上調⾄ 1,086 億美元；執⾏⻑戴蒙
表⽰正「略微收緊信貸標準」，稱部分信貸條款已過於寬鬆。
3   花旗第三季每股盈餘 2.85 美元、⾼於預期 2.73 美元，淨利約 52 億美元；但有形普通股權益報酬
率由第⼆季 13.0% 降⾄ 11.5% ，管理層預期第四季市場業務收⼊季減約 20% 。

4   花旗股價收跌 5.60% ⾄ 124.53 美元，為 7 ⽉以來最⼤跌幅；富國銀⾏淨利息收⼊與營收不及預
期、收跌 3.10% ，⾼盛營收 186.8 億美元為史上第⼆⾼，股價則收跌 0.23% 。
5   摩根⼤通、花旗、富國三⾏第三季淨利息收⼊合計 565 億美元，連續第五季創紀錄；三⾏不良
放款皆下降，顯⽰⾼利率尚未壓抑借貸與信⽤品質。
三⼤結論
1   銀⾏獲利由淨利息收⼊與交易業務⽀撐，⾼利率下借貸未減，佐證美國景氣仍在擴張；⾦融股
可⽤來分散半導體集中度，配置以資本充⾜的⼤型⾦控為主。
2   花旗財報優於預期卻因報酬率下滑與交易收⼊指引轉弱⽽重挫，顯⽰市場已從「獲利超預期」
轉向檢視下⼀季指引；當指引轉弱時，應優先減碼報酬率偏低、轉型中的銀⾏。
3   戴蒙收緊信貸標準是信⽤週期的早期訊號；當 10 年期殖利率站穩 5.2% 以上且信⽤利差擴⼤
時，應降低⾼收益債與⾼負債企業曝險，保留短存續期資產。
事件  3
10/13 胡塞⾶彈擊中沙烏地阿美達蘭總部，沙國聯軍空襲葉⾨燃油設施（台灣時

間  10/14 02:35 ）
五⼤重點

### Page 3

1   沙烏地國家通訊社引述能源部官員說明，沙烏地阿美位於達蘭的總部⼤樓 10/13 遭攻擊，引發
⼩規模⽕災、無⼈員傷亡；胡塞武裝事前已警告撤離阿美總部、利雅德煉油廠與利雅德機場。
2   胡塞的攻擊是對沙國聯軍空襲的報復：聯軍 10/13 空襲沙那⻄郊的燃油儲運設施，以及荷台達
省伊薩⾓沿岸燃油庫，胡塞稱造成 2 死 2 傷。
3   布蘭特原油 12 ⽉合約 10/13 結算每桶 105.69 美元，下跌 0.96 美元、跌幅 0.9% ； WTI 11 ⽉合約收
94.14 美元、上漲 0.43% ，美國取暖油期貨漲近 5% ⾄每加侖 4.92 美元。
4   美國總統川普 10/13 發⽂指控胡塞蓄意在期中選舉前推⾼油價；伊朗⾰命衛隊海軍同⽇稱⼀艘
關閉追蹤系統的油輪在霍爾⽊茲海峽東部起⽕受損。
5   以⾊列電視台 10/13 報導，以⾊列總理納坦雅胡近期與川普通話討論恢復對伊朗打擊；布蘭特
10/12 收盤 106.65 美元（背景），油價持續停留在百元以上。
三⼤結論
1   攻擊已從機場與油⽥延伸到阿美總部，但能源部稱無傷亡、未提及產量受損，油價當⽇反⽽⼩

跌，顯⽰市場對事件⾵險已有定價；原油維持事件避險，不作⻑期核⼼資產。
2   當布蘭特持續站穩每桶 100 美元以上且美國核⼼服務價格同步上⾏時，應預期聯準會 12 ⽉升息
定價升⾼，並縮短債券存續期間、降低⾼估值成⻑股。
3   成品油漲幅⼤於原油，對航空、運輸與化⼯成本壓⼒更直接；能源股可作通膨對沖，但優先低
成本、⾼⾃由現⾦流與⾼股息⿓頭，若霍爾⽊茲通航恢復則同步調降部位。
事件  4
10/13 南韓 KOSPI 暴跌 5.47% ， SK 海⼒⼠重挫 10.09% （台灣時間  10/13 14:32 ）
五⼤重點
1   南韓 KOSPI 指數 10/13 收跌 348.9 點、跌幅 5.47% ，收在 6,035.34 點，為 8 ⽉以來收盤新低、連
續第五⽇下跌；盤中觸發程式交易暫停（ sidecar ）與賣⽅斷路機制。
2   記憶體權值股領跌： SK 海⼒⼠收跌 10.09% ⾄ 144.3 萬韓元，三星電⼦跌逾 7% ；同⽇⽇經 225
指數收跌 1.82% ⾄ 67,777.52 點，韓元創 9 ⽉底以來最⼤跌幅。

3   南韓交易所資料顯⽰，今年 1 ⽉ 2 ⽇⾄ 10 ⽉ 12 ⽇外資在 KOSPI 累計賣超 199.10 兆韓元，其中三
星與 SK 海⼒⼠合計 173.20 兆韓元，占⽐ 87% 。

### Page 4

4   賣壓背景是中國⻑鑫存儲 DRAM 新架構進展（ 10/10 披露，背景），以及博通執⾏⻑陳福陽質疑
持續虧損的 AI 實驗室能否⻑期獲利，稱部分 AI 投資⼈「如同賭徒」。
5   南韓當局已警告將⼲預債市並把 10 ⽉公債發⾏計畫削減 5 兆韓元， 10 年期公債殖利率降⾄約
4.25% 、為六週低點，顯⽰資⾦由股市轉向債市避險。
三⼤結論
1   下跌主因是競爭預期與 AI ⽀出可持續性疑慮，⽽⾮記憶體報價轉弱；記憶體仍是 AI 供應鏈中議
價⼒最強的環節，配置以⿓頭為核⼼、⾼ Beta 為衛星並分批調整。
2   外資賣超⾼度集中在兩檔權值股，加上南韓家庭槓桿偏⾼，指數波動會被放⼤；持有韓股應控
制槓桿 ETF ，並保留部分美元避險，把股票部位與韓元曝險分開管理。
3   當 DRAM 與 NAND 平均售價先於需求下滑，或⻑約出現減量與延期時，應快速下修記憶體部
位；在報價與位元出貨仍同向成⻑前，股價回檔屬估值重置⽽⾮需求轉折。
事件  5
10/13 新台幣盤中貶 1% ⾄ 32.22 、創逾⼀年最⼤跌幅，外資賣超台股 1,653 億元

（台灣時間  10/13 14:35 ）
五⼤重點
1   新台幣 10/13 盤中⼀度貶值 1% ⾄ 1 美元兌 32.222 元，為 2025 年 6 ⽉以來最⼤單⽇跌幅；同⽇加
權指數收跌 960.65 點、跌幅 1.95% ，收在 48,341.31 點。
2   證交所資料顯⽰，外資 10/13 賣超上市股票 1,653.4 億元，連續第五⽇賣超，近 5 ⽇累計 3,130.9
億元；投信買超 238.7 億元，⾃營商賣超 569.8 億元。
3   櫃買指數跌 3.18% ⾄ 414.31 點，上市上漲 641 家、下跌 1,528 家，跌勢由權值電⼦擴散⾄中⼩型
股；外資前⼀交易⽇ 10/12 賣超 522 億元（背景）。
4   市場把新台幣⾛弱歸因於 AI 類股遭拋售、外資匯出，台積電是拖累⼤盤最多的個股；同⽇韓元
與 KOSPI 同步重挫，賣壓屬亞洲 AI 硬體市場共同現象。
5   融資餘額 10/12 為 1,187.4 萬張，位居近⼀年 95 百分位；市值加權本益⽐ 26.0 倍、預估本益⽐

21.9 倍，台積電第三季財報預定 10/15 公布。
三⼤結論

### Page 5

1   台灣出⼝與製造業循環仍在擴張，外資賣超屬⾼檔調節與 AI 評價重估，⽽⾮基本⾯轉弱；台股
維持核⼼配置，採拉回分批，並以內需與⾦融股降低半導體集中度。
2   融資餘額處近⼀年⾼位、外資連賣五⽇，若台積電財報指引不如預期，槓桿可能放⼤回檔；當
外資現貨與期貨同步偏空且美元續強時，應先降低槓桿與⾼ Beta 部位。
3   新台幣貶值反映資本匯出⽽⾮經常帳轉弱， 31 元只是觀察區⽽⾮⽬標；持有台股者適度分散
幣別，搭配美元短券部分對沖，不以單⼀匯率點位作為進出依據。
事件  6
10/13 應⽤材料公布 2030 年⽬標：營業利益率 40% ，股價跌 6.69% （台灣時間
10/14 00:46 ）
五⼤重點
1   應⽤材料 10/13 公布中⻑期⽬標： 2030 年⾮ GAAP ⽑利率 54% 、營業利益率 40% ， 2025 ⾄ 2030
年晶圓廠設備市占率提升逾 2 個百分點。
2   公司預估 2026 年服務收⼊成⻑逾 20% 、 2030 年突破 120 億美元，電⼦束設備組合 2026 年營收

20 億美元；⾄ 2030 年將⾃由現⾦流的 80% ⾄ 100% 回饋股東。
3   執⾏⻑迪克森表⽰，公司預期未來數年營收翻倍但員⼯⼈數不會同步增加， AI 已讓部分業務⽣
產率提升約 50% ；公司也將在 2030 年前把研發實驗室與製造產能擴⼤⼀倍以上。
4   股價收跌 6.69% ⾄ 464.24 美元，盤中最深跌 7.7% ；同⽇美光、科林研發同步⾛弱，費城半導體
指數盤中⼀度跌 3.02% ，終場收跌 0.23% ⾄ 12,208.80 點。
5   美股盤勢報導把半導體設備與記憶體股下跌連結到⻑鑫存儲技術進展與博通執⾏⻑對 AI ⽀出報
酬的質疑；公司本⾝未對股價反應發表說明。
三⼤結論
1   公司指引營收翻倍且利益率提升，代表先進製程與封裝帶動的設備需求仍有多年能⾒度；半導
體維持⾼配置，但估值擴張須以訂單、⽑利與⾃由現⾦流同步驗證。
2   ⻑期⽬標優異卻無法⽌跌，顯⽰市場已從成⻑敘事轉向檢視 AI 資本⽀出的報酬率；當雲端業者
資本⽀出維持⾼位但投⼊資本報酬率與⾃由現⾦流轉弱時，應先降低設備與⾼ Beta 硬體部位。

3   回饋 80% ⾄ 100% ⾃由現⾦流提⾼股東報酬下限，是現⾦流品質的正⾯訊號；配置上以具議價
權與現⾦流的設備⿓頭為核⼼，對只依賴資本⽀出敘事的標的降低權重。

### Page 6

事件  7
10/13 IMF 上修 2026 年全球成⻑⾄ 3.1% ，下修中國⾄ 4.5% （台灣時間  10/13
10:41 ）
五⼤重點
1   國際貨幣基⾦（ IMF ） 10/13 發布 10 ⽉《世界經濟展望》， 2026 年全球成⻑預測由 7 ⽉的 3.0% 上
修⾄ 3.1% ， 2027 年維持 3.4% 。
2   美國 2026 年維持 2.3% 、 2027 年由 2.2% 上修⾄ 2.3% ；南韓 2026 年由 2.6% ⼤幅上修⾄ 3.3% ，歐
元區由 0.9% 上修⾄ 1.1% ，⽇本 0.7% 。
3   中國 2026 年成⻑預測下修 0.1 個百分點⾄ 4.5% ；中國外交部同⽇回應，中國經濟基本⾯是好
的，對發展前景有信⼼。
4  IMF 說明上修主因是 AI 投資帶動的科技循環，以及供應鏈改道、庫存調節與財政⽀持抵銷部分
戰爭衝擊；⾸席經濟學家特雷羅稱經濟具韌性，但「韌性正接近極限」。
5  IMF 認為⾵險偏向下⾏，包括中東衝突延⻑、能源設施受損與 AI 獲利預期下修；精煉油品價格

⽐戰前⾼出 70% ⾄ 95% 。
三⼤結論
1  IMF 上修全球與南韓、下修中國，與「 AI 外需強、中國內需弱」的分化格局⼀致；全球景氣仍
屬擴張，股票優於⻑天期公債，但須以區域與產業分散，⽽⾮加碼同⼀種 AI 曝險。
2   中國下修呼應零售與⺠間投資疲弱，中國股市維持低權重、選擇性配置；當社會零售、⺠間投
資與房地產資⾦同步回升時，才考慮提⾼中國內需部位。
3  IMF 點名 AI 獲利預期下修是主要下⾏⾵險，與近期半導體賣壓相互呼應；當 AI 相關收⼊無法覆
蓋資本⽀出時，應降低⾼估值硬體，保留短券與⿈⾦作為防守。
事件  8
10/13 OPEC ⽉報上調 2027 年⽯油需求成⻑⾄每⽇ 255 萬桶（台灣時間  10/13
21:19 ）
五⼤重點
1   ⽯油輸出國組織（ OPEC ） 10/13 發布 10 ⽉《⽯油市場⽉報》，將 2027 年全球⽯油需求成⻑預測
由每⽇ 236 萬桶上調⾄ 255 萬桶。

### Page 7

2   ⽉報顯⽰沙烏地向 OPEC 申報 9 ⽉原油⽇產量增加約 200 萬桶⾄ 837.2 萬桶，較 8 ⽉逾 30 年低點回
升約三分之⼀，但仍明顯低於伊朗衝突前⽔準。
3  OPEC 同⽇表⽰將持續在 OPEC+ 框架內磋商，並強調除平衡供需外，確保能源運輸通道安全暢
通且具經濟可⾏性，對市場穩定同樣關鍵。
4  OPEC 秘書⻑ 10/12 表⽰今年⽯油需求成⻑為每⽇ 40 萬桶（背景）； 2027 年預測約為今年的六
倍，反映 OPEC 預期衝突後需求回補。
5   ⽉報公布後布蘭特當⽇仍收跌 0.9% ⾄每桶 105.69 美元，顯⽰短線油價由中東攻擊與運輸⾵險
主導，⽽⾮需求預測。
三⼤結論
1  OPEC 上調的是 2027 年需求、沙國產量則正從低點回升，代表供給恢復與需求回補將同時發
⽣；第四季油價情境區間每桶 80 ⾄ 110 美元，上緣由運輸中斷決定。
2   沙國增產若持續且霍爾⽊茲通航恢復， 2027 年⾼基期有助通膨回落；當運輸恢復、庫存回補
時，應降低原油部位，不把事件性油價當成⻑期需求成⻑。

3   需求預測上修對能源股獲利有⽀撐，但定位仍是通膨對沖⽽⾮核⼼成⻑資產；配置上優先低成
本、⾼⾃由現⾦流與⾼股息的⼤型能源業者。
事件  9
10/13 ⽇本 9 ⽉企業物價年增 6.8% ，低於預期 7.6% （台灣時間  10/13 07:50 ）
五⼤重點
1   ⽇銀 10/13 公布 9 ⽉國內企業物價指數（以 2020 年為 100 ）初值 135.7 ，年增 6.8% ，低於市場預
期與前值的 7.6% ，為 5 ⽉以來最⼩增幅。
2  9 ⽉企業物價⽉減 0.3% ，預期為⽉增 0.5% ，前值⽉減 0.2% ，連續兩個⽉下滑；⽇銀尚未公布
細項，降溫來源待細項確認。
3   ⽇銀總裁植⽥和男 10/13 在國會答詢表⽰，基礎通膨逐步上⾏、已⾮常接近 2% ，關鍵是讓物價
穩定在⽬標附近；⻑期利率原則上由市場決定。
4   ⽇銀同⽇公布的家計調查顯⽰， 88.6% 家庭預期⼀年後物價上漲（前次 90.4% ），⼀年後預期物
價漲幅中位數 10.0% ，通膨預期仍⾼。

### Page 8

5   有報導指⽇銀官員傾向 10/30 會議暫不連續升息，最早 12 ⽉再⾏動；⽇本 10 年期公債殖利率回
落⾄約 3% 、近三週低點，⽇圓升⾄約 158.1 。
三⼤結論
1   企業物價降溫但家庭通膨預期仍⾼，⽇銀可維持緩步升息、不急於連續⾏動；⽇債在 2027 年
度 143 兆⽇圓預算需求下仍不作核⼼避險。
2   ⽇圓仍在 158 附近，利差與財政擴張壓抑升值空間；持有⽇本資產宜分批並以選擇權管理匯率
波動，不因單⽉物價降溫預期⽇圓⼤幅⾛強。
3   ⽇股聚焦具定價權與海外市占的半導體設備⿓頭，降低單純出⼝ Beta ；當⽇銀明確放慢正常
化且⻑端殖利率回落時，再重新評估⽇債配置。
事件  10
10/13 華為：麒麟 9050 系列已量產，下⼀代麒麟 2027 晶⽚已就緒（台灣時間
10/13 15:07 ）
五⼤重點
1   華為 10/13 在終端創新技術溝通會表⽰，隨三摺疊⼿機 Mate XT 2 與旗艦 Mate90 Pro Max 推出
的麒麟 9050 系列晶⽚「在中國電⼦產業的託舉下」已成功量產。

2   華為半導體無線終端晶⽚產品管理部部⻑徐松餘表⽰， 2023 年以來搭載麒麟晶⽚的終端累計出
貨已超過 1 億台。
3   華為⾸度公布採「邏輯摺疊」設計的麒麟 9050 Pro 架構圖，整合⾃研 CPU 、 GPU 、達⽂⻄架
構 NPU 、 AI 影像處理器與數據機等模組。
4   徐松餘稱下⼀代麒麟 2027 晶⽚已就緒、測試結果符合預期，邏輯摺疊路線可⽀撐未來 10 年演
進，但未說明採⽤的製程節點。
5   中國⾼階⼿機晶⽚持續⾃製，與美⽅晶⽚出⼝管制並⾏；中美貿易休戰僅延⾄ 2027 年 1 ⽉ 10 ⽇
（背景），稀⼟與 H200 交貨仍有履約變數。
三⼤結論
1   華為以架構設計延續⾼階晶⽚⾃製，顯⽰中國在終端晶⽚的替代進度持續，但製程細節未公
開，實際性能與良率仍待驗證；不據此改變中國資產低權重、選擇性配置的判斷。

### Page 9

2   中國晶⽚⾃製進展屬終端產品層⾯，尚未改變先進製程與封裝的產能瓶頸；配置上仍以具技術
領先、全球客戶分散的台灣晶圓代⼯與先進封裝為核⼼，並以良率與訂單驗證。
3   中美休戰期限短且出⼝管制持續，當關稅、稀⼟或 H200 交貨出現履約變數時，應降低中國出
⼝導向與⾼度依賴中國營收的部位，只保留⾼技術製造題材。
待確認
1. 推算與⼝徑：事件 8 「 2027 年需求成⻑約為今年六倍」以 OPEC ⽉報 2027 年預測與 OPEC 秘書⻑所述今年每⽇ 40 萬
桶推算；事件 5 外資賣超 1,653.4 億元是否創單⽇紀錄未經證交所歷史資料核對。
2. 單⼀來源：事件 2 摩根⼤通「營收與淨利低於預期」、花旗第四季市場業務收⼊季減約 20% 指引，以及三⾏合計淨
利息收⼊ 565 億美元，為媒體整理，官⽅新聞稿僅核對到摩根⼤通淨利、每股盈餘與營收 502 億美元；三星電⼦跌幅
各來源介於 7.19% ⾄ 7.38% 。
3. 事件 1 ：威廉斯談話為媒體專訪，紐約聯儲官網未刊出講稿；柯林斯談話為即時紀錄。事件 3 ：阿美總部受損程度

未經公司證實，胡塞所稱傷亡為單⽅說法。
4. 事件 5 ：新台幣官⽅收盤價未取得， 32.222 為盤中低點；外資匯出歸因屬市場解讀。事件 6 ：應⽤材料⽬標為公司
活動之即時紀錄，股價下跌原因為盤勢報導歸因。

逐項核對。事件 9 ：⽇銀企業物價細項與降溫原因尚未取得；⽇銀暫緩 10 ⽉升息為媒體報導，⾮官⽅表態。事件 10 ：

1.40% ；澳洲央⾏ 9 ⽉會議紀要與英國央⾏委員格林、總裁⾙利偏鷹談話未調整利率，未單獨成件。

經濟緩步走揚，政治成德國股市新挑戰

德國財政體質較其他已發展國家良好 ， 且未來仍有擴張空間 ， 使經濟成長緩步復
甦且漸漸加速 。 市場樂觀預期德國DAX 指數 EPS 年增率將在 2027~2028 重返
雙位數，德股展望維持中性偏多。 
出刊日：2026/09/24 
273 期

### Page 2

2026

02-2771-6699 
 www.fubon.com 
台北市大安區仁愛路四段 169 號 
動見觀瞻

本期內容： 
1. 財政空間寬裕，
預算逐年擴張 
2. 經濟重返成長，
企業全球化布局 
3. 獲利成長重返雙
位數 
4. 極右翼崛起將成
歐洲大麻煩

重點摘要 
■ 德國公共債務占國內生產毛額比重為
64%， 財政體質相對穩健 ， 預算支出仍
持續增加，但短期再次大幅放鬆財政
限制的可能性不高。

■ 德國實質 GDP 自 2024 年第二季轉為
正成長，並連續 9 季維持成長，市場
期待基建及歐洲再武裝計畫帶動景氣。

■ 隨財政預算擴張 ， 市場預期2027 年及
2028 年每股盈餘年增率將回升至
15%及 13%，即使本益比因升息收斂
至長期平均 15 倍 ， 未來兩年股價仍有
4~18%的預期上漲空間。

■ 歐洲極右翼勢力的上升 ， 與移民 、 生活
成本、經濟停滯及對傳統政黨的不滿
有關；德國另類選擇黨支持率提高並
在東部邦選舉取得突破，若其影響力
持續擴大，可能削弱市場對德國歐盟
承諾的信心 、 影響外資流入 、 提高風險
溢價並拖慢改革進程。

德國 DAX 指數穩步走揚
